=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
An integer sequence where no two adjacent elements are the same is called a good sequence.
You are given two good sequences of length N: A=(A_1,A_2,\dots,A_N) and B=(B_1,B_2,\dots,B_N). Each element of A and B is between 0 and M-1, inclusive.
You can perform the following operations on A any number of times, possibly zero:

- Choose an integer i between 1  and N, inclusive, and perform one of the following:
- Set A_i \leftarrow (A_i + 1) \bmod M.
- Set A_i \leftarrow (A_i - 1) \bmod M. Here, (-1) \bmod M = M - 1.



However, you cannot perform an operation that makes A no longer a good sequence.
Determine if it is possible to make A equal to B, and if it is possible, find the minimum number of operations required to do so.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M
A_1 A_2 \dots A_N
B_1 B_2 \dots B_N

Output

If the goal is unachievable, print -1.
Otherwise, print the minimum number of operations required as an integer.

Constraints


- 2 \leq N \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 2 \leq M \leq 10^6
- 0\leq A_i,B_i< M(1\leq i\leq N)
- A_i\ne A_{i+1}(1\leq i\leq N-1)
- B_i\ne B_{i+1}(1\leq i\leq N-1)
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

3 9
2 0 1
4 8 1

Sample Output 1

3

You can achieve the goal in three operations as follows:

- Set A_1 \leftarrow (A_1 + 1) \bmod M. Now A = (3, 0, 1).
- Set A_2 \leftarrow (A_2 - 1) \bmod M. Now A = (3, 8, 1).
- Set A_1 \leftarrow (A_1 + 1) \bmod M. Now A = (4, 8, 1).

It is impossible to achieve the goal in two or fewer operations, so the answer is 3.
For example, you cannot set A_2 \leftarrow (A_2 + 1) \bmod M in the first operation, because it would make A = (2, 1, 1), which is not a good sequence.

Sample Input 2

3 9
1 8 2
1 8 2

Sample Output 2

0

A and B might be equal from the beginning.

Sample Input 3

24 182
128 115 133 52 166 92 164 119 143 99 54 162 86 2 59 166 24 78 81 5 109 67 172 99
136 103 136 28 16 52 2 85 134 64 123 74 64 28 85 161 19 74 14 110 125 104 180 75

Sample Output 3

811

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Maybe the flow model has additional constraints due to capacities [1,M-1] and modulo wrap? Wait edge differences e_i are modulo M, but represented 1..M-1. However, there is a subtlety: The operation at internal vertex k transfers one unit between e_{k-1} and e_k. But if e_{k-1}=M-1 and e_k=1, can we transfer from e_{k-1} to e_k? That would e_{k-1} +=? Let's map: x_k +=1 => e_{k-1} +=1, e_k -=1. If e_{k-1}=M-1, e_{k-1}+1=M (0) forbidden. If e_k=1, e_k-1=0 forbidden. So transfer direction from e_k to e_{k-1}? x_k -=1 => e_{k-1} -=1, e_k +=1. This is allowed if e_{k-1}>1 and e_k<M-1. So transfer from e_{k-1} to e_k? Actually e_{k-1} decreases, e_k increases: unit moves from left bin to right bin. So direction left->right corresponds x_k -=1. It is allowed only if source >1 and destination <M-1. Right->left corresponds x_k +=1, allowed if source <M-1 and destination >1. Thus transfer of a unit across boundary is allowed if source has at least one unit above lower bound and destination below upper bound. This is exactly moving "excess above 1" to fill "deficit below M-1"? Wait capacities [1,M-1]. If we think of each bin's height e, the amount of "space" above lower bound is e-1, space above? This is like moving chips (height above 1) from left to right, but destination must not overflow above M-1. Similarly moving right to left requires source e>1? Wait right->left: e_{j+1} decreases, e_j increases; requires e_{j+1}>1 and e_j<M-1. So in all cases, the bin losing a unit must have e>1, the bin gaining must have e<M-1. Now, min over c ≡ D mod M. We can implement binary search on k as described. Need compute F(c) for c maybe large negative/positive. S sorted list of length n. Use bisect_left.

Let's define function cost_at_c(c):
    idx = bisect_left(vals, c)
    left_sum = pref[idx]
    right_sum = total - left_sum
    return c*idx - left_sum + right_sum - c*(n-idx)
This works for all ints.

Now, let's think about the original operation "Set A_i ← (A_i + 1) mod M" and "Set A_i ← (A_i - 1) mod M" with (-1) mod M = M-1. Now, the flow conservation with h:

Then Δx_N = ? If f_{N-1}=+1 (token leaves bin to right), x_N -=1, so Δx_N = - f_{N-1}. For vertex1, f0 positive (token enters bin from left) corresponds x1 -=1, so Δx1 = -f0. Now, can any sequence of token moves with net f_j be realized with cost Σ|f_j|? The net f_j is the number of tokens crossing boundary j from left to right. If we choose a matching of surplus tokens to deficits/reservoirs, each token path crosses boundaries; total crossings = sum over boundaries of absolute net flow? Actually if tokens only move in net direction across each boundary, total crossings = sum |f_j|. But if some tokens cross a boundary left-to-right and others right-to-left, net f_j is difference; total crossings would be larger. The min cost for given net f_j is at least sum |f_j|, and we can achieve it by moving tokens monotonically from sources to sinks according to f_j. In 1D, if f_j is the net cumulative surplus, we can define for each unit of surplus at position p matched to a deficit at q>p (or reservoir), it crosses boundaries p..q-1 in positive direction; for q<p crosses negative. The total crossings per boundary equals |f_j| if matching is noncrossing. The standard matching of surplus/deficits in order yields f_j. Since initial and target configurations have at most C tokens per site, and f_j satisfies? Does it automatically satisfy that the number of tokens that need to cross a boundary in a given direction doesn't exceed capacity? Capacity is per site, not per boundary; moving tokens one at a time may temporarily fill a site. But the standard "adjacent swaps" with indistinguishable tokens and capacity C can be done in exactly sum |prefix diff| by repeatedly moving leftmost surplus token to leftmost deficit; this never violates capacity because it's equivalent to sorting a multiset? Let's reason.

For total tokens conserved and no reservoirs, h^A, h^B with 0≤h≤C. The minimum adjacent moves is sum |prefix difference|. This is a known result for "minimum cost to transform one configuration into another with capacity C" because tokens are indistinguishable and each move changes prefix sums by at most 1. The lower bound: For each prefix, the number of tokens that must cross boundary j is at least |H_j^A - H_j^B|, where H_j are prefix counts. Sum over j gives lower bound. Achievability: Move tokens across boundary j whenever prefix difference positive/negative; can schedule by scanning left to right, maintaining capacity. This is like bubble sort. With reservoirs, same by adding dummy reservoir sites with infinite capacity at ends; prefix sums include reservoir? The formula with f0 chooses net exchange with reservoirs. We can model reservoirs as sites 0 and N with infinite capacity, initial/final token counts maybe variable; the same prefix-difference lower bound and achievability holds for a path with reservoirs if we allow moving tokens between reservoir and adjacent site. Pseudo:

import sys, bisect

def solve():
    input = sys.stdin.readline
    N,M = map(int,input().split())
    A = list(map(int,input().split()))
    B = list(map(int,input().split()))
    if M == 2:
        print(0 if A == B else -1)
        return
    # edge differences
    a = [(A[i+1] - A[i]) % M for i in range(N-1)]
    b = [(B[i+1] - B[i]) % M for i in range(N-1)]
    # prefix S
    S = [0] * N
    pref = 0
    for i in range(N-1):
        pref += a[i] - b[i]
        S[i+1] = pref
    # S[0]=0
    vals = sorted(S)
    n = N
    total = sum(vals)
    pref_sum = [0]*(n+1)
    for i,v in enumerate(vals):
        pref_sum[i+1] = pref_sum[i] + v

    def cost(c):
        idx = bisect.bisect_left(vals, c)
        left_sum = pref_sum[idx]
        right_sum = total - left_sum
        return c*idx - left_sum + right_sum - c*(n-idx)

    D = (B[0] - A[0]) % M
    minS = vals[0]
    maxS = vals[-1]
    k0 = (minS - D) // M
    lo = k0 - 1
    # ceil division
    k1 = -((-(maxS - D)) // M)
    hi = k1

    def g(k):
        return cost(D + k*M)

    def diff(k):
        return g(k+1) - g(k)

    # binary search first k in [lo, hi] with diff(k) >= 0
    l = lo
    r = hi
    while l < r:
        mid = (l + r) // 2
        if diff(mid) >= 0:
            r = mid
        else:
            l = mid + 1
    ans = g(l)
    print(ans)

Need ensure lo <= hi. Let's check if maxS-minS could be 0 and D residue not in interval, k0 and k1 differ by 1. lo = k0-1, hi=k1. If k1 = k0+1, lo<hi. If D≡S, k1=k0, lo=k0-1<hi. So yes lo<hi always? Suppose minS=maxS and D? k0=floor((S-D)/M), k1=ceil((S-D)/M). If S-D is integer multiple of M, k0=k1, lo=hi-1. If not, k1=k0+1, lo=hi-1? Example S-D=5,M=10: k0=0,k1=1,lo=-1,hi=1 -> lo<hi. If S-D=-5: k0=-1,k1=0,lo=-2,hi=0. So lo<hi. Good.

But wait, if maxS-minS >0, k1 could be less than k0? No, maxS>=minS, so ceil((maxS-D)/M) >= floor((minS-D)/M)? Yes. lo = k0-1 < k0 ≤ k1 = hi, so lo<hi. Good.

Now, binary search first diff>=0. We know diff(lo)<0, diff(hi)>0. But if hi = lo? Not possible. If hi=lo+? At least 1. Good.

If D=0,S=5,M=10. k0=0,lo=-1; k1=ceil(5/10)=1,hi=1. l=-1,r=1. mid=0. diff(0)=g(1)-g(0)=50-50=0 >=0, r=0. l=-1,r=0. mid=(-1+0)//2=-1. diff(-1)=g(0)-g(-1)=50-150=-100<0, l=0. ans g(0)=50. Good.

Now, cost function with bisect_left on vals sorted. If c is huge, idx=n, right_sum=0, cost = c*n - total. If c < min, idx=0, left_sum=0, cost = total - c*n. Good.

If M small, M=3, s_i ∈[-1,1], max-min≤N, /M≈N. So O(N). Binary search log ~18. Good.

Need ensure code reads input robustly. Use sys.stdin.buffer.read().split() for speed. Let's implement with iterator.

Code:

import sys, bisect

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    it = iter(data)
    N = next(it); M = next(it)
    A = [next(it) for _ in range(N)]
    B = [next(it) for _ in range(N)]
    if M == 2:
        print(0 if A == B else -1)
        return
    S = [0]*N
    pref = 0
    for i in range(N-1):
        a = (A[i+1] - A[i]) % M
        b = (B[i+1] - B[i]) % M
        pref += a - b
        S[i+1] = pref
    vals = sorted(S)
    n = N
    total = sum(vals)
    pref_sum = [0]*(n+1)
    for i,v in enumerate(vals):
        pref_sum[i+1] = pref_sum[i] + v

    def cost(c):
        idx = bisect.bisect_left(vals, c)
        left = pref_sum[idx]
        return c*idx - left + (total - left) - c*(n - idx)

    D = (B[0] - A[0]) % M
    minS = vals[0]
    maxS = vals[-1]
    k0 = (minS - D) // M
    lo = k0 - 1
    k1 = -((-(maxS - D)) // M)
    hi = k1

    def g(k):
        return cost(D + k*M)

    def diff(k):
        return g(k+1) - g(k)

    l = lo
    r = hi
    while l < r:
        mid = (l + r) // 2
        if diff(mid) >= 0:
            r = mid
        else:
            l = mid + 1
    print(g(l))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Let's write final code cleanly:

import sys
from bisect import bisect_left

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N, M = data[0], data[1]
    A = data[2:2+N]
    B = data[2+N:2+2*N]

    if M == 2:
        print(0 if A == B else -1)
        return

    S = [0] * N
    pref = 0
    for i in range(N-1):
        ea = (A[i+1] - A[i]) % M
        eb = (B[i+1] - B[i]) % M
        pref += ea - eb
        S[i+1] = pref

    vals = sorted(S)
    n = N
    total = sum(vals)
    pref_sum = [0] * (n + 1)
    for i, v in enumerate(vals):
        pref_sum[i+1] = pref_sum[i] + v

    def cost(c):
        idx = bisect_left(vals, c)
        left = pref_sum[idx]
        right = total - left
        return c * idx - left + right - c * (n - idx)

    D = (B[0] - A[0]) % M
    mn = vals[0]
    mx = vals[-1]

    k0 = (mn - D) // M
    lo = k0 - 1
    k1 = -((-(mx - D)) // M)
    hi = k1

    def g(k):
        return cost(D + k * M)

    def diff(k):
        return g(k + 1) - g(k)

    l, r = lo, hi
    while l < r:
        mid = (l + r) // 2
        if diff(mid) >= 0:
            r = mid
        else:
            l = mid + 1

    print(g(l))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

=== ANSWER ===
. The final answer should be code block only. The above repetitive thoughts are internal; final must be concise. I'll provide the Python code.

Double-check code one more time for potential bug: In `cost`, if `idx` is from bisect_left, left_sum = pref_sum[idx] is sum of elements < c. Elements >= c count n-idx. right_sum = total - left_sum. Formula correct. If c is very large, idx=n, left_sum=total, cost = c*n - total. If c is very small, idx=0, right_sum=total, cost = total - c*n. Good.

Potential bug: `pref` variable in S building: We set S[0]=0, then for each edge i, pref += a-b; S[i+1]=pref. This yields S_j for j=1..N-1. Good.

Potential bug: `D = (B[0] - A[0]) % M`. For M>=3. Good.

Potential bug: `k1 = -((-(mx - D)) // M)`. If mx-D is positive, e.g. 113, (-113)//182 = -1, -(-1)=1. Good. If mx-D=0, (0)//182=0, -0=0. Good. If mx-D=-1, (1)//182=0, -0=0. Good.

Potential bug: `lo = k0 - 1`. If k0 is very negative, lo okay. `hi = k1`. If hi maybe less than lo? no.

Potential bug: In binary search, if `diff(mid)` calls `g(mid+1)` and `g(mid)`, and mid=hi, g(hi+1) maybe c beyond maxS+M, cost positive. Good.

Potential bug: If `l` and `r` are negative, `mid = (l+r)//2` floors. Standard. Example l=-2,r=-1: mid=-2? (-3)//2 = -2. If diff(-2)<0, l=-1; if diff(-2)>=0, r=-2. But can first true be -2? diff(lo)<0, so no. But if lo=-2, diff(-2)<0, so l will become -1. Good. If l=-1,r=0: mid=-1. diff(-1) maybe? If diff(-1)>=0, r=-1, loop ends ans -1. If diff(-1)<0, l=0. Good.

Potential bug: If `hi` is negative? maxS maybe negative? S values can be negative, but maxS >= minS. D in [0,M-1]. k1=ceil((maxS-D)/M) could be negative if maxS < D. Example minS=maxS=-100, D=0,M=10: k0=floor(-100/10)=-10, lo=-11; k1=ceil(-100/10)=-10, hi=-10. lo<hi. Binary search works with negative. diff(lo)<0, diff(hi)>0? If maxS=-100, c1=D+k1*M = -100 = maxS, diff(hi)=F(-90)-F(-100)? Wait c1=maxS, interval [maxS, maxS+M] all >= maxS, derivative N, diff=N*M >0. Good.

Potential bug: If `maxS-D` is negative and k1 computed as 0 for -1, c1=D > maxS? Example maxS=-100,D=0,M=10, maxS-D=-100, k1=ceil(-10)=-10, c1=-100. Good. If maxS=-1,D=0,M=10, k1=ceil(-0.1)=0, c1=0 > maxS. Good.

Potential bug: The cost function with c maybe not in sorted vals but bisect_left handles.

Potential bug: For M=2 special, if A==B but B good, output0. If A!=B output -1. Good.

Now, final code.</think>```python
import sys
from bisect import bisect_left


def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N, M = data[0], data[1]
    A = data[2:2 + N]
    B = data[2 + N:2 + 2 * N]

    # M = 2: every good sequence is alternating, and no operation is possible.
    if M == 2:
        print(0 if A == B else -1)
        return

    # S[j] = sum_{i=1..j} ((A[i+1]-A[i]) - (B[i+1]-B[i])) mod M, as integers
    S = [0] * N
    pref = 0
    for i in range(N - 1):
        ea = (A[i + 1] - A[i]) % M
        eb = (B[i + 1] - B[i]) % M
        pref += ea - eb
        S[i + 1] = pref

    vals = sorted(S)
    n = N
    total = sum(vals)
    pref_sum = [0] * (n + 1)
    for i, v in enumerate(vals):
        pref_sum[i + 1] = pref_sum[i] + v

    def cost(c):
        idx = bisect_left(vals, c)
        left_sum = pref_sum[idx]
        right_sum = total - left_sum
        return c * idx - left_sum + right_sum - c * (n - idx)

    # Need c == B[0] - A[0] (mod M)
    D = (B[0] - A[0]) % M

    mn = vals[0]
    mx = vals[-1]

    # Search range for k where c = D + k*M
    k0 = (mn - D) // M
    lo = k0 - 1

    # ceil((mx - D) / M)
    k1 = -((-(mx - D)) // M)
    hi = k1

    def g(k):
        return cost(D + k * M)

    def diff(k):
        return g(k + 1) - g(k)

    # g is convex on integers. Find first k where diff(k) >= 0.
    l, r = lo, hi
    while l < r:
        mid = (l + r) // 2
        if diff(mid) >= 0:
            r = mid
        else:
            l = mid + 1

    print(g(l))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
